# ES event study (pre-registered): «Preço × CVD Normalizado [Order Flow]»

Generated by `study_es.py` on 2026-10-11 08:59 UTC. Every number comes from the CSV files of this folder (names at the end). The results contain aggregate statistics only, no price data.

## Summary and interpretation

Written after the pre-registered run, from the tables below, and revised after an external review. The plan, the
inputs and the primary family were not changed after the outcomes were seen. Section 10 lists the departures from the
plan. The review corrected the matched-control implementation and relabelled the cost p-values; section 11 holds the
post-hoc sensitivities it asked for. `CHANGELOG.md` lists every change and how each number moved. Words such as
"hint" describe numbers that do not survive multiple testing. They are not findings.

**Primary endpoint (ES 5m, signed σ return at 30 min, 16 classes): null.** No class survives Benjamini–Hochberg at
5% (all q = 1.000). None reaches even raw p < 0.05; the smallest p is 0.100 (continuation-up resolution, −0.130 σ).
The 16 class means lie between −0.130 σ and +0.041 σ; in basis points they range from −1.56 to +0.97 bps (the two
ranges come from different classes). Among the non-zero outcomes, 46.2–55.6% are in the signal's direction (no
information = 50%). The pre-registered hit rate, which counts the 3.3–6.6% of exact zeros (tick grid) as misses, is
44.5–52.0%, so a random-direction trade would score about 47–48% on it. The day-block bootstrap CIs are about
±0.04–0.05 σ for the frequent classes (flow-led Z states, pivots), ±0.10 σ for absorption, and ±0.13–0.17 σ for the rare
classes (pinned, price without flow, resolutions). Entry is the first print after the alert bar closes, a zero-latency
upper bound; with a 1- or 2-minute delay the endpoint stays null (0 of 16 under BH, smallest p 0.102 and 0.188;
section 11.3).

**Power, relative to costs.** A 30-min round trip costs 0.13–0.22 σ (0.325 pt, section 6). With 80% power the study
would have detected true means of 0.05–0.07 σ for the flow-led Z states and pivots and about 0.14 σ for absorption.
Those are smaller than the cost, so a tradeable effect in these classes is effectively ruled out. For the price-led Z
states the MDE is 0.13–0.14 σ, about the size of the cost. For pinned, price without flow and resolutions it is
0.19–0.25 σ, also about the cost: an effect just large enough to pay the round trip could have been missed there. The
MDE is a post-hoc column.

**Secondary analyses: consistent with chance.**
- ES 5m, all horizons: 5 of 64 class × horizon cells have raw p < 0.05, against about 3.2 expected by chance. The
  smallest q in the table is 0.28.
- ES 15m: 4 of 48 cells (about 2.4 expected), smallest q 0.30. At 30 min on 15m, buy absorbed shows +0.111 σ
  (p 0.011) and pinned buy +0.143 σ (p 0.019), both q 0.150, not significant.
- The 15m numbers are not a direction signal. Sell absorbed at 30 min is −0.069 σ in its own direction, so the price
  rose after both absorption sides. Against the matched controls (corrected design), buy absorbed is +0.077 σ
  (p 0.12) and pinned buy +0.139 σ (p 0.049, q 0.32). Section 11.2 locates both: they are significant only for events
  whose window contains the 14:59 CT settlement minute, and they vanish against exact-slot controls (see the post-hoc
  note below).

**Does the CVD add information beyond price? No detectable directional information.**
- *Twins, absorbed vs confirmed.* An absorption (flow shock, price not following) was followed by the same 30-min
  return as a same-size flow shock that did move the price: difference +0.015 σ, p 0.68, bootstrap CI
  [−0.051, +0.084] on 5m; −0.016 σ, p 0.64 on 15m. The flow-versus-price condition that defines the label changes
  nothing measurable in the signed return that follows.
- *Twins, price without flow vs price shocks with flow.* On 5m: +0.136 σ at 5 min and +0.128 σ at 15 min (p 0.017–0.018,
  q 0.109, not significant). At 30 min the difference is +0.028 σ (p 0.61). This is a short-lived hint that price
  moves without flow carry on slightly more than price moves with flow. It is not confirmed, and it is below the cost
  at those horizons (0.33–0.53 σ at 5 min, 0.19–0.31 σ at 15 min).
- *Matched random controls* (same session type, 30-min bucket, year, σr tercile within the year, sign of pE and
  tercile of |pE|; all events of the same class removed from the cells; SE including the sampling error of the control
  means). At 30 min no class differs from its matched controls after BH: 0 of 18 on each timeframe. On 5m none
  reaches raw p < 0.05 (smallest 0.086). On 15m two do (pinned buy +0.139 σ, p 0.049; continuation-up +0.142 σ,
  p 0.042; q 0.32 for both).
- *Matched on the K-bar price leg* (post hoc). The pre-registered cells match the 15-minute move pE, not the K-bar leg
  P_K that defines the Z classes. Matched instead on 12 bins of P_K, the Z classes stay null on 5m: +0.010, +0.017,
  −0.039 and −0.009 σ (p ≥ 0.10); no class survives BH on either timeframe (section 11.1).
- *The strongest number in these tables* comes from the twin sets on 5m at 5 min. Flow-confirmed shocks are −0.042 σ
  and price shocks with flow −0.041 σ against their matched controls (n ≈ 27,000 and 42,000, p ≤ 0.0004). After the
  matched-design correction they pass BH within the matched table (q 0.013–0.014; before: −0.037 and −0.033 σ,
  q 0.056). That table is a secondary family, so this is a flag, not a finding. It is a tiny 5-minute reversal after
  large flow-confirmed moves, about a tenth of the 5-minute cost (0.33–0.53 σ). It belongs to large shocks in general
  (both twin sets), not to an indicator signal. On 15m the twin sets drift slightly the other way (+0.02 to +0.03 σ at
  30 min, p 0.06–0.07).
- *Unsigned moves (volatility).* Descriptive; the event-versus-twin comparison was added after the review and is
  post hoc.
  - On 5m at 30 min, 13 of the 16 classes show an unsigned excess over their matched controls of +0.06 to +0.18 σ,
    10 of them with p < 0.05. Flow-led Z≤−2, pinned buy and pivot top are near zero. The twin sets show an excess too:
    +0.08 σ for confirmed flow shocks and +0.09 σ for price shocks with flow, both p < 0.001.
  - On 15m, absorbed and pinned events show no excess: +0.001, +0.022, −0.042 and +0.053 σ, p ≥ 0.32.
  - Whether the CVD condition itself changes the excess is mixed. Absorbed minus confirmed, matched excess: +0.061 σ
    on 5m (p 0.012), −0.163 σ on 15m (p < 0.001). Price without flow minus price with flow: +0.041 σ on 5m (p 0.27),
    +0.072 σ on 15m (p 0.22).
  - The unmatched gaps are larger, because time of day and volatility account for much of them. Price without flow
    minus price with flow: +0.22 σ on 5m, +0.19 σ on 15m (p ≤ 0.001). Absorbed minus confirmed: +0.07 σ on 5m,
    −0.28 σ on 15m.
  - So large flow or price shocks are followed by somewhat larger moves (METODOLOGIA 9.1: imbalance relates to
    volatility, not direction). The data do not show that the CVD conditions consistently add to that, and on 15m the
    absorption label marks smaller moves than a confirmed shock.

**Gate.**
- On ES with the 1-minute candle-rule delta, ρ̂ is high: median 0.71 on 5m and 0.66 on 15m. P(ρ̂ < 0.30) is 1.9% and
  0.9%, and the gate is open on 98.1% and 99.1% of hqOk bars. The 1-minute estimate inflates ρ, as METODOLOGIA 6.5
  notes.
- The gate is therefore almost never binding. Turning it off adds at most 27 events to a class on 5m, and ρmin 0.40
  removes at most 58. Every variant is null (0 of 16 under BH in each).
- The added and removed subsets are too small to say anything: n of 1 to 58, nothing interpretable. One cell has
  p 0.013, but n = 2.
- Point (d) of the audit (does the gate hide relevant regimes?) cannot be settled on this ES configuration, because
  the gate almost never closes.

**Costs (0.325 pt per round trip, $16.25: a lower bound).**
- The pre-registered cost fits a 1-lot in normal RTH liquidity. It is optimistic at the open, the 15:00 CT
  settlement, the release bars and the 17:00 CT reopen, where many signals cluster; in the thin 2008–2012 overnight
  book; and in the 2008 and 2020 stress periods. Section 6 adds a 2-tick stress cost (0.575 pt). No news-calendar or
  FOMC filter was applied.
- On 5m at 30 min, all 16 "trade s" rules are net negative (−0.04 to −0.89 pt). Three opposite rules are net positive:
  fading the two continuation resolutions and held-down, +0.09 to +0.24 pt. None is significant (two-sided p ≥ 0.42),
  and all three turn negative at the stress cost.
- Across all 224 rule cells (class × horizon × direction, both timeframes), no net expectancy is significantly above
  zero. On a one-sided test of net > 0 the smallest p is 0.028, and every BH q-value is 1.000. The cost tables'
  p-value columns are two-sided tests of net = 0 (renamed p_net_eq0 / p_opp_net_eq0); a small value there usually
  means significantly negative.
- The best single cell is pinned sell at 60 min on 15m: +1.05 pt net, one-sided p 0.028, n 385. It survives no
  correction, and it is not the same population as the 30-minute cell (n 543, −0.07 pt net). Most of these events sit
  on the 14:45 and 15:00 CT bars. Their 60-minute windows run past the session end unevenly by era: 81% survive in
  2008–12, 24% in 2013–20 and 84% in 2021–26 (section 11.5).
- In σ units the same cell nets only +0.03 σ (p 0.66). Point means weigh the later, higher-priced years more; the σ
  columns are the scale-free view.

**Stability.**
- Per-year signs are close to a coin toss: 6–11 positive years out of 19 on 5m (sign-test p ≥ 0.17), 7–14 on 15m
  (p ≥ 0.06).
- Both halves (2008–2016, 2017–2026) are null.
- A few RTH/ETH and σr-tercile cells reach raw p < 0.01; none survives BH (q ≥ 0.25 on 5m, ≥ 0.15 on 15m).
  - On 5m: flow-led Z≤−2 in ETH −0.082 σ (also −0.082 σ against matched controls, p 0.006); held-down in ETH −0.25 σ.
  - On 15m: buy absorbed in 2008–2016 +0.199 σ and in RTH +0.132 σ.

**Z calibration on ES.**
- P(|Z| ≥ 2) is 6.47% on 5m and 5.75% on 15m; P(|Z| ≥ 3) is 1.43% and 0.98% (normal: 4.55% and 0.27%). This is
  stable across the halves and every year (6.1–6.8% per year on 5m) and close to METODOLOGIA 6.8 (5.4–6.2% on BTC).
  Mean Z ≈ 0.
- Z is not calibrated by time of day. P(|Z| ≥ 2) is 8.9% in RTH vs 5.5% in ETH on 5m, and 9.4% vs 4.3% on 15m
  (RMS Z 1.16 vs 0.96 on 5m), because the RMS is pooled over the 23-hour Globex session.

**Post-hoc observation: absorbed and pinned events at the cash close (section 11.2; not pre-registered).**
- Absorbed and pinned events cluster at the cash close and the open. On 15m, 58% of absorptions and 67% of pinned
  events fall on the 14:45 and 15:00 CT bars; the 14:45 bar alone holds 564 of 1,267 buy and 519 of 1,286 sell
  absorptions. On 5m the most frequent absorption bars are 15:00, 08:30 and 15:05 CT.
- **Mechanism (hypothesis).** The 14:59 CT minute is the CME settlement window and the NYSE closing auction. Its share
  of the clean-session volume grew from 1.3% in 2008 to 2.9–4.2% from 2017 on. Under the TradingView Auto candle rule
  the whole volume of that one 1-minute candle goes to one side, so a window that contains it gets a large |Δ| whose
  side is set by a single candle.
  - The window contains the 14:59 minute for 40.4–49.2% of absorbed and pinned events on 15m and 16.6–20.4% on 5m.
  - In those events the side equals the sign of the 14:59 candle-rule delta in 99.6–100% of cases.
  - So these labels are at least partly an artefact of the delta estimator, not only of intraday volume seasonality.
  - The fidelity study agrees. On the 2026 TopstepX ES sample the 14:59 minute holds 6.6% of the volume, and the
    1-minute correlation between the 1-minute and 1-second candle-rule deltas is 0.36 with that minute and 0.64
    without it. The 5m flow-event recall of the 1-minute candle rule against the true aggressor delta is 0.13.
- **What it does to the outcomes.** The significant part of the 15m secondary hints sits in these settlement-window
  events, and the post-close drift accounts for it.
  - Buy absorbed with the 14:59 minute in its window: +0.133 σ (p 0.003). Pinned buy: +0.189 σ (p 0.003).
  - Against exact-slot controls (same bar of the day) those become +0.012 σ (p 0.86) and −0.035 σ (p 0.79).
  - Without the settlement window: +0.094 σ (p 0.19) and +0.096 σ (p 0.35). The point estimates are similar, on half
    the events, and not significant.
  - The price rose after both sides: sell absorbed in the settlement window is −0.073 σ in its own direction on 15m
    and −0.117 σ (p 0.005) on 5m.
  - The unconditional 30-min drift from the 15m 14:45 bar is +0.051 σ (p 0.002), against +0.027 σ (p 0.28) from 14:30.
    On 5m it is +0.037 to +0.046 σ from 14:50 and 14:55 (p ≤ 0.004), against −0.005 σ from 14:30. The 30-minute
    matching bucket mixes these slots, which is why the exact-slot control is the sharper test here.
  - On 5m, without the settlement window, absorbed and pinned events are null: |mean| ≤ 0.045 σ, p ≥ 0.44.
- Close-clustered windows often span the pre-2021 15:15–15:30 CT halt (`halt_share`, up to 28% on 15m), and their
  60-minute windows run past the session end unevenly by era (section 11.5).

**Other post-hoc caveats.**
- *Resolutions after the daily break.* A pending absorption's life is counted in bars, so it survives the daily break
  and weekends, and the 17:00 CT reopen gap can trigger its "continuation" or "held" alert. That covers 78 of 2,636
  resolution alerts on 5m (3.0%; 60 on the session's first bar) and 146 of 1,758 on 15m (8.3%; 126 on the first bar).
  Excluding them changes nothing material: on 5m continuation-up is −0.133 σ (p 0.10); on 15m it is +0.139 σ (p 0.06).
  Section 11.4.
- *Data scope.* The study uses clean front-month, dominant-contract sessions; roll, roll-window and non-dominant
  sessions are left out. A TradingView user on ES1! keeps those sessions, and the indicator's σΔ, ρ and RMS of D then
  differ for a few sessions around each roll. The null covers non-roll weeks only.

**For users of the indicator (guidance; the indicator source was not changed by this study).**
- Treat absorbed and pinned alerts whose 15-minute window contains the 14:59 CT minute as unvalidated: with the
  TradingView Auto delta their side is set by one candle. An indicator option to flag or suppress them would be a
  natural follow-up.
- Pending absorptions could expire at the session end, or gap-driven resolutions could be labelled.
- The timings above assume CME real-time data on TradingView; with 10-minute-delayed data the 5- and 15-minute
  horizons are gone before the alert arrives.
- Chart the dominant contract (or a volume-rolled continuous contract) and treat roll-week alerts as unvalidated.

**Bottom line.**
- On 18.7 years of ES front-month data with the TradingView-Auto delta (tier 2, 1-minute candle rule), none of the
  indicator's 16 signal classes predicts the signed return 30 minutes later. The primary endpoint is 0 of 16 after FDR,
  and none reaches even raw 5%; this holds with a 1–2 minute entry delay.
- Nothing in the secondary analyses survives multiple testing except a tiny 5-minute reversal after large,
  flow-confirmed shocks. That is not an indicator signal and is about a tenth of the cost. No naive rule earns money
  after costs. The CVD conditions add no detectable directional information beyond matched price moves.
- Large flow and price shocks are followed by somewhat larger moves. Whether the CVD conditions add to that is
  inconsistent between 5m and 15m.
- The 15m hints are significant only for absorbed and pinned events at the 14:59 CT settlement minute, where the
  1-minute candle rule assigns the whole closing-auction volume to one side. Against exact-slot controls they vanish:
  they are post-close drift.
- This agrees with METODOLOGIA 9.1 (the Z is a descriptive meter; events at most a slight short-term effect) and with
  the earlier BTC result, where the continuation after absorption disappeared with 1-minute estimated delta.
- The study does not test signals built on true aggressor delta (tier 3). ES 1-minute data cannot provide it, and the
  2013 tick sample (15 sessions) is far too short for this design.

## 1. Data, settings and signals

- **ES 5m**: 1,280,221 bars, 4,688 clean sessions, 2008-01-02 → 2026-09-28; ke = 3 bars, life = 12 bars. Valid outcome windows: 5 min 1,275,414, 15 min 1,266,040, 30 min 1,251,979, 60 min 1,223,857. Share of valid windows that span an intra-session halt: 5 min 0.00%, 15 min 0.49%, 30 min 1.23%, 60 min 2.07%.
- **ES 15m**: 427,128 bars, 4,688 clean sessions, 2008-01-02 → 2026-09-28; ke = 1 bars, life = 4 bars. Valid outcome windows: 15 min 422,341, 30 min 417,655, 60 min 408,283. Share of valid windows that span an intra-session halt: 15 min 0.00%, 30 min 0.64%, 60 min 1.64%.
- Delta: candle rule on 1-minute intrabars, side carried over the whole 1-minute stream (TradingView "Auto" on an intraday chart), summed per chart bar: quality tier 2 (medium). Indicator inputs: defaults (N 100, K 20, M 1000, clip 4, thresholds 2/3, ρmin 0.30, event window 15 min, life 60 min, zF 2, pinned 3/0.5) with pivots on (L5 R2, distance 5–60, magnitude 0.5).
- Signals = the indicator's 14 alertcondition sets, with the two Z alerts split by the dominant leg (16 classes). Signal at the close of the bar where it is known; entry at the next open; exit at the close n bars after the signal bar. s = flow side (absorbed, pinned), price side (price without flow), leading leg (Z states), direction of the resolving close (resolutions), −1 top / +1 bottom (pivots). Positive = continuation of s.
- Primary outcome: s·10⁴·ln(C[t+n]/O[t+1]) / (σr·√n), σr = the indicator's lagged one-bar σ (bps) at the signal bar. "σ" below always means these units. A random-direction trade has expectation 0 σ; the drift of the unconditional long trade is in section 4 (baseline).
- Entry timing: O[t+1] is the first trade after the alert bar closes, i.e. a zero-latency upper bound. A TradingView alert gated on the bar close reaches a trader seconds later, and a manual order later still; users without CME real-time data on TradingView see bars delayed by 10 minutes, which uses up the 5- and 15-minute horizons. Entry delays of 1 and 2 minutes are in section 11.3 (post hoc).
- Data scope: front-month, dominant-contract sessions only (the clean sessions of the 1-minute file: 75 roll sessions, 30 non-dominant-contract sessions and 35 roll-window sessions are left out; manifest es_1m.sessions). A TradingView user on ES1! keeps those sessions in the stream and, depending on the continuous-contract roll setting, may chart the expiring contract for part of roll week while its volume collapses; σΔ, ρ and the RMS of D (M = 1,000 bars, about 3.6 sessions on 5m) then differ from the study's for a few sessions around each roll. Outcome windows never cross a session or a contract change. The results cover non-roll, dominant-contract weeks.

## 4. Controls: does the CVD add information beyond price?

**Conditional twins.** Absorbed (flow shock, price not following) vs CONFIRMED flow shocks (|zF| ≥ 2, s·zP ≥ |ρ|·|zF|); price without flow vs price shocks WITH flow (|zP| ≥ 2, sign(zP)·zF > 0). The twins are built as indicator events (gate open, cluster start per side, refractory ke observations). diff = event − twin (σ), two-group regression, SE clustered by day; CI_boot = day-block bootstrap of the difference. A second table (added after the review, post hoc, secondary) compares the UNSIGNED moves: the unmatched difference of mean |σ| and the difference of the matched |σ| excess (|σ| minus the |σ| of the matched controls, each set against its own cells), with a day-clustered SE that includes the control-mean error.

**Matched random controls.** Cell = year × RTH (08:30–15:15 CT) / ETH × 30-min CT bucket × σr tercile within the year × sign of pE × tercile of |pE|. Control = mean over the cell's candidate bars (≥ 5) after removing every event of the same class (or twin set), signed with the event's s. diff = event − control (σ); |σ| diff = |event| − control |σ| (volatility effect). se includes the sampling error of the control means (se ctrl fixed: without it, the original computation). Rows "twin:*" are the twin sets, for reference. Design corrected after the review (section 10, CHANGELOG.md).

**Unconditional baseline.** All candidate bars (hqOk, gate open, σr > 0, pE defined, valid window), long direction.

## 5. Gate study

Variants re-derived by the engine on the same bars (Z, zF, zP and their calibration do not depend on the gate). Signed σ return at 30 min per variant; BH within each variant's 16 classes (flag only). "added" = events of the variant on bars where the default has none of that class; "removed" = the converse. Other horizons per variant are in `gate_{tf}m.csv`.

## 6. Costs: naive rules (round trip 0.325 pt = $16.25 per contract)

The pre-registered cost of 0.325 pt (1 tick of slippage + about $4 commission) is a LOWER BOUND: it fits a 1-lot in normal RTH liquidity, measured against trade prints. It is optimistic where these signals cluster (the 08:30 CT open, the 15:00 CT settlement, the 07:30 and 09:00 CT release bars, the 17:00 CT reopen), in the thin 2008–2012 overnight book and in the 2008 and 2020 stress periods; retail all-in commissions are often $4–5 per round trip. A stress column at 0.575 pt (2 ticks + commission) is added (post hoc). No news-calendar or FOMC filter was applied: recurring release times are absorbed only by the 30-minute matching bucket.

gross = mean s·(C[t+n] − O[t+1]) in points; net = gross − 0.325; opp net = −gross − 0.325 (the opposite rule); p net / p opp = clustered TWO-SIDED test of net = 0 (columns p_net_eq0 / p_opp_net_eq0; a small p can mean significantly NEGATIVE); p>0 / q>0 = one-sided test of net > 0 and its BH q-value over all full-sample rule cells of both timeframes and both directions (224 cells); net 2t = net at the 0.575 pt stress cost; valid = share of the class's signals with a valid window at that horizon (long horizons lose the events near the cash close to the session end, unevenly by era: section 11.5, so the population changes with the horizon); cost σ = mean cost in σ-units; net σ = mean(σ return − cost σ), the scale-free version (point means weigh the later, higher-priced years more). Overlapping trades are all counted (no position management).

## 9. Implementation choices (fixed before any outcome was computed; the matched-control item as corrected after the review, section 10)

- Event classes are the indicator's own alertcondition sets (alZUp/alZDn split by the dominant leg, alAbsB/S, alPinB/S, alPwfU/D, alResCB/CS/HB/HS, pivTop/pivBot): events while the zF/zP calibration is weak (evWeak) and resolutions of weak events are not alerts and are not studied. On the ES files this removes no event (the calibration is complete long before the first event).
- Horizons are counted in chart bars, as pre-registered. Windows are dropped only when they cross a session break (session_date changes) or a contract change. Windows that span an intra-session trading halt (mainly the 15:15–15:30 CT halt that existed until 2021) are kept; their share is reported (column halt_share).
- σ-unit normalisation uses sr_p at the signal bar (σr of the N previous observations, the σ the indicator used on that bar) and √n with n the horizon in bars. Bars with sr_p ≤ 0 (e.g. the 2020-03-09 limit-locked market) are excluded.
- Hit rate = share of signed returns > 0 (the pre-registered definition); the share of exact zeros (tick grid) is reported separately. Because exact zeros count as misses, a random-direction trade scores below 50% on it. hit_nz (added after the review) = share of positive returns among the non-zero ones, whose no-information value is 50%.
- Clustered SE: CR1 with G/(G−1) small-sample factor, clusters = trading days (session_date), two-sided p from a t distribution with G − 1 df. Bootstrap: days with at least one event resampled with replacement, percentile interval; 10,000 replicates for the primary endpoint, 2,000 elsewhere; seeds derived from the cell name.
- Twins: CONFIRMED and PRICE-WITH-FLOW events are built with the indicator's machinery (gate open, z defined, cluster start per side with a refractory period of ke observations, not evWeak), the same as the absorbed and price-without-flow classes they are compared with. Sides pooled (main row) and per side. The comparison is a two-group regression with a dummy, CR1 clustered by day.
- Matched controls (as corrected after the review; see section 10): a cell is (year of the trading day, RTH 08:30–15:15 CT vs ETH, 30-minute CT clock bucket, σr tercile WITHIN THE YEAR, sign of pE, tercile of |pE|). Candidate control bars: hqOk, gate open with the default ρmin, sr_p > 0, pE defined, valid window at the horizon. The |pE| terciles are computed over all candidates of the full sample (pE is already in σ-units); the σr terciles within each year (σr is in bps, and its level moves by a factor of several across years). Every event of the same class (for a twin set: of the same set) is removed from the cells, not only the event itself. Each event's control is the MEAN over the remaining bars of its cell (the expectation of a random matched draw), signed with the event's s. Events whose cell has fewer than 5 control bars are dropped from this comparison (count reported). The SE includes the sampling error of the control means: each control bar contributes its influence (weight of its cell in the event average / bars in the cell × its deviation from the cell mean) to its own day, and the event and control contributions are clustered together by day (CR1). se_ctrl_fixed is the SE with the control means treated as known (the original computation). The bootstrap resamples all trading days, recomputing the event mean and moving the control means through their per-day influence.
- Unconditional baseline: all candidate control bars (same filters), long direction (s = +1); the short-direction mean is its negative.
- Gate study: the variants are produced by re-running the validated engine on the same bars with the gate input changed (Params.gate / rho_min). This is the exact re-derivation from the zF/zP/ρ/Z series, because Z, zF, zP and their calibration do not depend on the gate; only the classes, clusters, resolutions and Z alerts do. Pivots do not depend on the gate. "Added" events = variant events of a class on bars where the default has no event of that class; "removed" = the converse.
- Costs: 0.325 pt per round trip subtracted from every event (naive rule, overlapping positions allowed); the opposite rule pays the same cost. The cost is also expressed in σ-units of each event (0.325 / (O_entry·σr·√n·1e−4)).
- Multiple testing outside the primary family: Benjamini–Hochberg q-values are computed within each secondary table (its own family) and reported only as a flag; nothing outside the primary family is confirmatory.

## 10. Deviations from the plan

- Gate study procedure: the ρmin 0.20 / 0.40 and gate-off variants were re-derived by re-running the validated engine on the same bars with the gate input changed, instead of with separate code that re-derives the classes from the stored zF/zP/ρ/Z series. Reason: the two are identical by construction (Z, zF, zP and their calibration do not depend on the gate; only the classes, clusters, resolutions and Z alerts do), and the engine is the validated implementation of the cluster and resolution machines. Checks: the default re-run reproduces the 29 stored signal columns exactly, and the cluster routine used for the twins rebuilds alAbs and alPwf exactly.
- Matched controls procedure: instead of drawing a finite random sample of matched control bars per event, each event's control is the mean over ALL candidate bars of its matching cell (leaving the event bar out), i.e. the expectation of a random matched draw. Reason: same estimand, no Monte Carlo noise. Events whose cell holds fewer than 5 control bars are dropped from that comparison (counts in the table, 0.1–9% of events).
- Post-hoc additions (descriptive, after the results were seen; no test, no effect on any pre-registered number): the time-of-day concentration table of the events (`tod_*.csv`, section 1) and the minimum detectable effect column of the primary table (MDE80 = 2.80 × clustered SE: two-sided 5% test, 80% power).
- Correction after review, matched controls (control (2) of the plan; the plan itself is unchanged): (a) all events of the same class are now removed from the control cells (before: only the event bar itself; on 15m the same-class events were about 8% of the controls of absorbed and pinned events, which pulled event − control toward 0); (b) the σr tercile is now taken within each year (before: full-sample cut points, so within a year-specific cell σr was matched only coarsely; the plan says "σr tercile" without fixing the reference set, and the robustness study already used within-year terciles); (c) the SE and the bootstrap now include the sampling error of the control means (before: treated as fixed, which understated the SE by about 5–6% for the rare 15m classes). The stability table's matched column uses the same design. The σr-tercile STABILITY split keeps the full-sample cut points (volatility regimes in absolute terms, as pre-registered).
- Relabelled after review, costs: the p-value columns of the cost tables were named p_net_gt0 / p_opp_net_gt0 but held two-sided tests of net = 0; they are now p_net_eq0 / p_opp_net_eq0. Added: one-sided p of net > 0 (p_net_gt0_1s, p_opp_net_gt0_1s) with BH over all full-sample rule cells of both timeframes (q_net_1s, q_opp_net_1s); valid_share (share of signals with a valid window at that horizon); a stress cost of 0.575 pt (2 ticks + commission). The pre-registered cost (0.325 pt) and its net numbers are unchanged.
- Post-hoc additions after review (secondary or descriptive, section 11; none enters the primary family): hit rate among non-zero outcomes (hit_nz); in the twin table, the unsigned |σ| comparison of each set with its twin (unmatched difference and difference of the matched |σ| excess); a P_K-matched control (year × RTH × 30-min bucket × 12 bins of the K-bar price leg P_K) and an exact-bar-slot control; the settlement-minute sensitivity of absorbed and pinned events (window containing the 14:59 CT minute or not, without the most frequent open / close bars, side vs the 14:59 candle) with the slot drift and the 14:59 volume share; entry latency of 1 and 2 minutes; resolutions of absorptions born in an earlier session; window survival by era.

## 11. Post-hoc sensitivities (added after the external review)

Everything in this section was added after the results and the review were seen. It is descriptive or sensitivity analysis: nothing here is confirmatory, and none of it changes a pre-registered number. q-values, where shown, are BH within the table slice named in the text (a flag only).

### 11.1 Alternative matched controls (30 min)

Same machinery as section 4 (same-class events removed, control-mean error in the SE), other cells. **P_K bins**: year × RTH/ETH × 30-min bucket × 12 quantile bins of the K-bar price leg P_K, the leg that defines the Z classes (the pre-registered cells match the 15-minute move pE, not P_K). **Exact bar slot**: the pre-registered cells with the exact bar of the day instead of the 30-minute bucket (the post-close drift differs between slots of the same bucket: 11.2). q = BH over the 18 sets of each design.

### 11.2 The 14:59 CT settlement minute and absorbed / pinned events (30 min)

Mechanism (a hypothesis, not tested as such): the 14:59 CT minute (CME equity-index settlement window, NYSE closing auction) carries a large and growing share of the session volume (table below). Under the TradingView Auto candle rule the WHOLE volume of that one 1-minute candle is given to one side, by the sign of its close − open. An event window that contains this minute therefore gets a large |Δ| whose side is set by a single candle, and a flow shock (|zF| ≥ 2) with little price move: an "absorption" or "pinned" label that is at least partly an artefact of the delta estimator, not only of intraday volume seasonality. The fidelity study points the same way: on the TopstepX ES 2026 sample the 14:59 CT minute holds 6.6% of the volume, and the 1-minute correlation of the candle-rule delta with the 1-second candle-rule delta is 0.36 with that minute and 0.64 without it; on the 2013 tick sample the 5m flow-event recall of the 1-minute candle rule against the true aggressor delta is 0.13 (±ke bars). Events whose window (the ke bars ending at the event bar) contains the 14:59 minute are compared with the others, and the most frequent open / close bars are dropped; side = sign(14:59) is the share of the in-window events whose side equals the sign of that session's 14:59 candle-rule delta.

Share of the clean-session volume traded in the 14:59 CT minute, by year: 2008 1.3%, 2009 1.5%, 2010 1.5%, 2011 1.6%, 2012 1.8%, 2013 2.1%, 2014 2.0%, 2015 2.7%, 2016 2.6%, 2017 4.2%, 2018 3.0%, 2019 3.1%, 2020 2.9%, 2021 3.4%, 2022 3.0%, 2023 3.3%, 2024 3.6%, 2025 3.7%, 2026 4.0%.

### 11.3 Entry latency (30 min)

Entry at the close of the 1st or 2nd one-minute intrabar after the alert bar closes, instead of the first print (delay 0, the plan). Exit, σ scale and windows unchanged. drift = mean s·(delayed entry − O[t+1]) in points: how far the price moved in the signal's direction while waiting (positive = a worse entry for the "trade s" rule). ### 11.4 Resolutions of absorptions born in an earlier session

The life of a pending absorption is counted in bars, so it survives the daily maintenance break and weekends; the 17:00 CT reopen gap can then break H0/L0 and trigger a "continuation" or "held" alert that a trader would not read as a flow resolution (and the reopen book is the thinnest of the day). Signed σ return at 30 min; first bar = resolutions on the first bar of the session. All horizons in `prior_res_{tf}m.csv`.

### 11.5 Window survival by era

Share of the signals whose outcome window is valid at 60 min (the longest horizon), by era. The session-end layout changed over the sample (in these data the last bar of the session opens at 16:15 CT until 2012, 16:00 in 2013–14 and 15:45 from 2016; the 15:15–15:30 CT halt removes the 15:15 bar until mid-2021), so a 60-minute window that starts near the cash close crosses the session end in some eras and not in others: for events clustered at the close, the 60-minute cell is a different population from the 30-minute cell.
